STANDATA interpretation 23-BCI-026/23-FCI-014 
Building/Fire  
Single-Egress-Stairs (SES) for buildings of residential occupancy  
Date Issued: October 2026 
Page 1 of 7 
Purpose 
To clarify that compliance with the National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) can be achieved via 
alternative solutions (variance) that will achieve at least the minimum level of performance required by Division B 
in the areas defined by the objectives and functional statements attributed to the applicable acceptable solutions .  
Where a single-egress-stair (SES) building does not comply with the acceptable solutions of Division B, this 
STANDATA clarifies that it is possible for the building to comply with the NBC(AE) via alternative solutions .  
Discussion 
With growing demands to densify urban areas to address affordable housing, designers are exploring different 
methods to satisfy the requests of developers and building owners. One such design that enables smaller 
residential lots in built-up urban areas to be fully utilized is the SES building. This style of building challenges the 
conventional multi-unit residential building design (comprised of two or more egress stairs), while still supporting 
multiple dwelling units in a building. 
Commonly referred to a “SES” building, some designers identified such buildings as a potential answer to the 
“missing middle” (a concept that highlights a lack of medium-density housing options). Alberta cities need 
medium-density housing throughout their urban areas, while these urban areas also contain vacant lots not large 
enough to construct conventional, high-density residential buildings. Designers consequently propose this as an 
opportunity to solve an urban planning and housing problem via smaller-footprint SES buildings. 
Change is being sought to introduce prescriptive requirements for SES buildings into a future edition of the 
National Building Code of Canada. Until such changes are es
---Application---
This interpretation applies to buildings of residential occupancy that require two or more egress stairs as per the 
acceptable solutions of Division B of the NBC(AE), where the alternative solution path permitted by Sentence 
1.2.1.1.(1)(b) of Division A of the NBC(AE), is chosen by the applicant to demonstrate that a single-egress-stair 
(SES) design complies with the NBC(AE) and the Act. 
Interpretation 
1. An AHJ may accept a residential occupancy building of SES design (where the requirements of Division B 
require two or more exit stairs), provided the following conditions are met:  
• The documentation submitted to support the alternative solution is in conformance with Section 2.3. of Division 
C of the NBC(AE). 
• The design provides for an approximately equivalent or greater safety performance required by Division B of the 
NBC(AE) in the areas defined by the objectives and functional statements attributed to the applicable 
acceptable solution(s). 
 
2. To ensure ongoing Code compliance following occupancy, the owner must have the issued variance 
documentation available on the premises for review by the fire SCO to verify the conditions under which the 
variance was issued have not been contravened. 
 
 
 
This INTERPRETATION is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta.  
 
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are to Division B of the National Building Code – 
2023 Alberta Edition and Division C of the National Fire Code – 2023 Alberta Edition 
Issued by the Provincial Building and Fire Administrators 
 
 
 
[Original Signed]                                                                 [Original Signed] 
Paul Chang      Tina Parker 
Provincial Building Administrator    Provincial Fire Administrator

Email: safety.services@gov.ab.ca Phone: 1-866-421-6929 
To sign up for our List Subscription Service: municipalaffairs.gov.ab.ca/am_list_subscription_services 
 
---Interpretation---
1. An AHJ may accept a residential occupancy building of SES design (where the requirements of Division B 
require two or more exit stairs), provided the following conditions are met:  
• The documentation submitted to support the alternative solution is in conformance with Section 2.3. of Division 
C of the NBC(AE).

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-030 =====

STANDATA interpretation 23-BCI-030/23-FCI-012 
Building/Fire  
  
Measures to mitigate fire spread to adjacent buildings  
Date Issued:  March 2025 
Page 1 of 10 
Purpose 
To clarify the requirements for measures to mitigate fire spread to adjacent buildings , water supply, and access 
for firefighting as per the National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) and the National Fire Code – 
2023 Alberta Edition (NFC(AE)).   
Discussion 
The NBC(AE) has specific provisions for firefighting under Subsection 3.2.5. such as fire access routes and water 
supply requirements. Additionally, Part 8 addresses safety measures at construction and demolition sites and 
references the NFC(AE). 
In the NFC(AE), Section 5.6., “Construction and Demolition Sites,” and Section 2.8., “Emergency Planning,” 
specify that fire safety plans are mandatory requirements to be prepared in cooperation with the fire department 
and the authority having jurisdiction (AHJ), such as a building safety codes officer (building SCO) or fire safety 
codes officer (fire SCO). In municipal areas that are unaccredited in the fire discipline, fire safety plans must be 
accepted in writing by the building SCO and the fire department.  In municipal areas that are accredited in the 
building and fire disciplines, these plans are reviewed by both disciplines; however, it is typically the fire SCO that 
provides the acceptance of the final plans.   
Prior to the construction, alteration, or demolition of a building, the owner, developer, or contractor who is 
responsible for a construction or demolition site is required to submit the mandatory fire safety plan to  the AHJ. 
The measures outlined in the fire safety plan should provide a reasonable reassurance that fire hazards  are 
removed and/or controlled on the site, so that fires that originate in a building or facility undergoing construction or 
demolition will not quickly spread to exposed adjacent buildings or fa
---Application---
This interpretation applies to all construction and demolition sites that range from multi-storey buildings to 
single-storey residences. Extra considerations should be provided for buildings with irregular configurations or 
special uses. As this STANDATA does not incorporate all the language from the NBC(AE) and NFC(AE), it is the 
responsibility of all parties to ensure all other applicable legislation are implemented and adhered to.  
STANDATA 23-BCI-030/23-FCI-012  Page 8 of 10 
 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
Fire STANDATA | Alberta.ca   
 
©2025 Government of Alberta | March 2025 | Municipal Affairs 
  
Interpretation 
For explanatory purposes, this interpretation provides recommendations for both active and passive systems to 
mitigate fire spread to adjacent buildings.  
Measures to Mitigate Fire Spread to Adjacent Buildings 
Most of these suggested solutions are adaptable or scalable and could be used in multiple applications. The 
degree of application should be determined in advance in conjunction with the SCO/AHJ  following a risk 
assessment. Firefighting capabilities that are available to a municipality in an emergency should also be taken 
into account when considering protection solutions. 
NOTE: NFC(AE) reference Note A-5.6.1.2. provides a non-exhaustive, non-exclusive list of suggestions which 
notes the following items: 
• spatial separation; 
• water curtains; 
• fire resistive construction methods; or 
• temporary fire barriers such as a fire tarpaulin. 
 
Methods that building owners and/or contractors could utilize to provide some of the required protection include, 
but are not limited to, the following: 
• ensure adequate water supply for firefighting operations is provided as soon as combustible construction 
materials arrive on the site; 
• application of an acceptable intumescent or flame-retardant coating in accordance with the 
manufacturer’s directions and the criteria utilized in any performance tests; 
• install an acceptable temporary flame/heat-resistant membrane affixed to the exterior sheathing of 
exposed walls prior to closure and cladding in accordance with the manufacturer’s directions and the 
criteria utilized in any performance tests; 
• complete all fire separations, including installation of fire-rated doors, as each floor is completed, 
• cover exposed combustible materials as soon as possible; 
• provide tem
---Interpretation---
For explanatory purposes, this interpretation provides recommendations for both active and passive systems to 
mitigate fire spread to adjacent buildings.  
Measures to Mitigate Fire Spread to Adjacent Buildings 
Most of these suggested solutions are adaptable or scalable and could be used in multiple applications. The 
degree of application should be determined in advance in conjunction with the SCO/AHJ  following a risk 
assessment. Firefighting capabilities that are available to a municipality in an emergency should also be taken 
into account when considering protection solutions. 
NOTE: NFC(AE) reference Note A-5.6.1.2. provides a non-exhaustive, non-exclusive list of suggestions which 
notes the following items: 
• spatial separation; 
• water curtains; 
• fire resistive construction methods; or 
• temporary fire barriers such as a fire tarpaulin. 
 
Methods that building owners and/or contractors could utilize to provide some of the required protection include, 
but are not limited to, the following: 
• ensure adequate water supply for firefighting operations is provided as soon as combustible construction 
materials arrive on the site; 
• application of an acceptable intumescent or flame-retardant coating in accordance with the 
manufacturer’s directions and the criteria utilized in any performance tests; 
• install an acceptable temporary flame/heat-resistant membrane affixed to the exterior sheathing of 
exposed walls prior to closure and cladding in accordance with the manufacturer’s directions and the 
criteria utilized in any performance tests; 
• complete all fire separations, including installation of fire-rated doors, as each floor is completed, 
• cover exposed combustible materials as soon as possible; 
• provide temporary sprinkler piping with open sprinkler heads that is connected to the standpipe installed 
on each floor; 
• temporarily fence and secure a building site; 
• provide on-site security personnel tasked with regular fire watch duties; 
• install security lights around the construction area; 
• designate certain outdoor areas for smoking; 
• limit the amount of combustible and flammable materials stored on site; 
• ensure that combustible and flammable materials are properly separated when stored on site ; 
• ensure that daily clean up and disposal of combustible waste are in secured non-combustible containers; 
• provide a fir

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-032 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca  
Fire STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2025 Government of Alberta  |  July 2025  |  Municipal Affairs 
  
STANDATA interpretation 23-BCI-032/23-FCI-005 
Building/Fire  
  
Occupancy classification for buildings used for tank vehicles 
Date Issued:  July 2025 
Page 1 of 3 
Purpose 
To clarify the application of the National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) and the National Fire 
Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NFC(AE)) to buildings used for parking, repairing, and/or servicing of tank vehicles. 
Discussion 
Tank vehicles containing flammable liquids or vapours, or combustible liquids or vapours present a unique hazard 
inside a building. In addition to the hazard inherent in the product itself, there is a potential for the release of 
flammable or combustible vapours in significant quantities which can overwhelm a standard ventilation system for 
a building and become a fire or explosion risk. 
The activity of bringing a tank vehicle into a building involves specific references from both the NBC(AE) and the 
NFC(AE). The Provincial Administrators for Building and Fire consider a joint interpretation necessary to 
communicate the importance of applying this interpretation to both new building construction and practices within 
existing buildings. The NBC(AE) applies to: 
1. new buildings prior to occupancy; or 
2. existing buildings undergoing a change in occupancy. 
The NFC(AE) applies to the operation and maintenance of existing buildings and establishes requirements for 
tank vehicles. 
Code References 
NFC(AE) Division A, Article 1.4.1.2.  
1.4.1.2. Defined Terms 
Authority having jurisdiction means a safety codes officer in the fire discipline exercising authority pursuant 
to designation of powers and terms of employment in accordance with the Safety Codes Act . 
Building means any structure used or intended for supporting or sheltering any use or occupancy . 
Combustible liquid means a liquid having a flash point at or above 37.8°C and below 93.3°C. (See 
Subsection 4.1.2. of Division B.) 
Flammable liquid means a liquid havi
---Application---
This interpretation applies to buildings used for any one or all of the following activities: parking, repairing, and 
servicing of tank vehicles. 
Interpretation 
1. Buildings used for parking tank vehicles containing flammable or combustible gases or vapours are classified 
as Group F, Division 1, high hazard industrial occupancies and must: 
 
• be specifically designed for that purpose according to both the NBC(AE) and the NFC(AE ); 
• be provided with a ventilation system in accordance with Part 6 of the NBC(AE);  
• have electrical systems installed in conformance with the electrical code regulation made pursuant to the Safety 
Codes Act; 
• have fuel-fired appliances in service rooms with no openings between the hazardous location and the 
components capable of igniting a flammable or combustible vapour; and 
• have fuel-fired appliances installed in conformance with the applicable codes and standards, and in the case of 
gas-fired appliances, in conformance to the gas code regulation made pursuant to the Safety Codes Act. 
Note: Additional requirements of Part 3 or Part 6 of the NBC(AE) or Part 4 of the NFC(AE) may apply.  
 
2. Buildings to be used for parking, repairing, and servicing tank vehicles that have been purged of their flammable 
or combustible gases or vapours are classified as Group F, Division 2, medium hazard industrial occupancies, 
provided: 
 
• the area is specifically designed for that purpose; 
• all flammable liquids and combustible liquids have been removed and the tanks are purged in accordance with 
Section 4.11. of the NFC(AE); 
• there is a ventilation system in accordance with Part 6 of NBC(AE); 
• electrical systems are installed in conformance with the electrical code regulation made pursuant to the Safety 
Codes Act; 
• fuel-fired appliances are installed in service rooms and have no openings between the garage location and the 
components capable of igniting a flammable or combustible vapour unless the appliances are at a height as 
permitted by the applicable codes or regulation; and 
• fuel-fired appliances installed in conformance with the applicable codes, and in the case of gas-fired appliances 
in conformance to the gas code regulation made pursuant to the Safety Codes Act. 
 
This INTERPRETATION is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta.  
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are

This INTERPRETATION is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta.  
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are to Division B of the National Fire Code – 2023

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-034 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca  
©2025 Government of Alberta  |  March 2025  |  Municipal Affairs 
 
 
  
STANDATA interpretation 23-BCI-034 
Building  
  
Elevator operation and accessibility for residential buildings  
Date Issued:   March 2025 
Page 1 of 4 
Purpose 
This is to clarify the application of the National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) for elevators to be 
operational in residential buildings required to have a barrier-free path of travel.  
Discussion 
Municipal Affairs and authorities having jurisdiction have received numerous complaints from residents that their 
buildings have no working elevators over long periods of time, placing residents with mobility issues with impeded 
access to or from their residential units. This poses serious health and safety concerns for both seniors and 
persons with disabilities. Safety Codes Officers (SCOs) have indicated to Municipal Affairs that there is 
uncertainty with respect to safety codes concerning the authority to direct an owner to take all necessary steps to 
place an elevating device into operation. 
The NBC(AE) requires at least one elevator to be available for firefighter use at all times for high- rise buildings, 
but this requirement does not directly apply to access and use by building occupants. The Elevating Devices 
Code Regulation (which includes the elevating devices codes) sets rules for the safety of operational elevating 
devices, but does not require an elevating device to be operational.  
The authority for ordering an owner to make an elevator operational is under Article 3.8.2.3. of the NBC(AE). 
Where a residential building has a requirement for a barrier-free path of travel and is served by a passenger-type 
elevator or other platform-equipped passenger-elevating device, an operational elevating device is necessary to 
maintain a barrier-free path of travel and compliance with Article 3.8.2.3. Without an operational elevating device, 
a barrier-free path of travel is not maintained, and therefore, the building is no longer in compliance with the 
NBC(AE).  
Sentence
---Application---
This interpretation applies to residential buildings where a barrier-free path of travel is required and is served by a 
passenger-type elevator(s) or other platform-equipped passenger-elevating device(s). 
 
Interpretation 
This interpretation provides clarification that a building SCO has the authority under the Safety Codes Act to 
enforce the NBC(AE) and require the owner to make the elevating device operational or put in place alternative, 
equivalent measures for accessibility. The requirement for operational elevating devices to support a barrier-free 
path of travel came into force on May 30th, 1981, and is applicable from May 30th, 1981, and onward to all 
buildings with elevating devices.  
This interpretation does not mean that an elevating device cannot be taken out of service for maintenance or 
repairs or because of an emergency. Under reasonable situations, where the owner is diligently attempting to 
have maintenance or servicing carried out, any enforcement action by the SCO including an order for compliance 
may not be necessary. In situations where the owner is not cooperating in response to a complaint , or purposely 
or negligently delaying or preventing the operation of the elevating device(s), a building  SCO may issue an order 
to the owner to place the elevating device(s) in operation and set conditions and timelines for compliance.  
 
This INTERPRETATION is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta.  
 
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are to Division B of the National Building Code – 
2023 Alberta Edition 
Issued by the Provincial Building Administrator and the Provincial Barrier-Free Administrator

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-035 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca

STANDATA interpretation 23-BCI-035 
Building  
  
Attached garage separation serving a house with a secondary suite 
Date Issued:  M arch 2025 
Page 1 of 4 
Purpose 
To clarify the application of Sentence 9.10.9.18.(3) of the National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition 
(NBC(AE)) to a house with a secondary suite. 
Discussion 
Sentence 9.10.9.18.(3) of the NBC(AE) waives the requirement for a one-hour fire separation between an 
attached garage and the remainder of the building where the attached garage serves “only the dwelling unit” to 
which it is attached or in which it is built. This has created debate as to whether Sentence 9.10.9.18.(3) can be 
applied to a house with a secondary suite, as the attached garage is considered to serve not only the primary 
dwelling unit, but the secondary suite as well. This is the case regardless of the actual garage access/use that the 
secondary suite tenant is granted by the owner. 
 
A secondary suite is defined in the NBC(AE) as a “self-contained dwelling unit,” and the definition’s appendix note 
states that a house with a secondary suite contains two dwelling units in total (see Sentence 1.4.1.2.(1) of 
Division A).   
 
However, while the NBC(AE) defines a house with a secondary suite as containing two dwelling units, it is 
important to consider the following: 
 
• T he NBC(AE) already addresses the separation between an attached garage and a single dwelling unit or  
house with a secondary suite, for the purposes of limiting fire spread from the garage to other parts of the 
building (see Sentences 9.35.1.1.(1) and 9.35.4.1.(2)). 
 
• T he NBC(AE) treats a house with a secondary suite the same as a single dwelling unit for the purposes of other 
requirements, such as for exposing building face construction for a house that faces its detached garage (see 
Sentence 9.10.15.5.(4)) or determining whether professional involvement is required for a project (see 
Sentence 2.4.1.1.(2) of Division C). 
 
• T he NBC(AE) permits 
---Application---
This STANDATA applies where an attached storage garage, containing five motor vehicles or fewer, serves a 
house with a secondary suite. 
Interpretation 
Sentence 9.10.9.18.(3) applies where an attached storage garage serves only the dwelling unit, or house with a 
secondary suite, to which it is attached or in which it is built.  
 
 
 
 
This INTERPRETATION is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are to Division B of the National Building 
Code – 2023 Alberta Edition. 
Issued by the Provincial Building Administrator

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-037 =====
 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
Electrical STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
 
©2025 Government of Alberta | April 2025 | Municipal Affairs 
 
   
STANDATA interpretation 23-BCI-037/24-ECI-009 
Building/Electrical  
Lightning protection systems 
Date Issued: April 2025 
Page 1 of 3 
Purpose 
To clarify the requirements for installing, altering, or adding to lightning protection systems. This clarification is for 
building owners, operators, designers, and electricians, as well as safety codes officers (SCOs) in the building 
and electrical disciplines. 
Discussion 
The National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) provides requirements for lightning protection 
systems. The Designated Trades and Restricted Activities Regulation classifies lightning protection systems as an 
electrical system. The Permit Regulation requires an electrical permit to install, alter, or add to an electrical 
system. 
Some questions brought forward are the following: 
1. Which adopted codes reference the CSA B72 standard? 
• Sentence 3.6.1.3.(1) of the NBC(AE) requires lightning protection systems conform to CAN/CSA -B72-M87, 
“Installation Code for Lightning Protection Systems.” Rule 10-108 (Appendix B) of the 2024 Canadian Electrical 
Code (CE Code) lists the CSA-B72 standard as a recommended practice for the installation of lightning 
protection systems. 
 
2. Which edition of the CSA B72 standard applies? 
• The 2024 CE Code references the latest edition, which is CSA B72:20, “Installation code for lightning protection 
systems.” 
 
3. What are the requirements for professional involvement? 
• Section 18 of the Administrative Items Regulation allows an SCO to request professional involvement for 
electrical systems if, in the opinion of the SCO, there are special safety concerns due to the size or complexity i f 
the project.  
 
4. What are the requirements for a permit? 
• Section 43(1) of the Designated Trades and Restricted Activities Regulation defines a lightning protection 
system as an electrical system. As per Section 18 of the Permit Regulation, an electrical permit is re
---Application---
This interpretation applies to all structures where a lightning protection system is installed.  
 
INTERPRETATION 
1. The installation, alteration, or addition to a lightning protection system is required to comply with the 
CAN/CSA-B72:20 standard. 
 
2. An electrical permit is required for the installation, alteration, or addition to a lightning protection system; a  
building permit is not required. 
 
 
This INTERPRETATION is applicable throughout the province of Alberta 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Issued by the Provincial Building and Electrical Administrators

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-038 =====
Elevating Devices STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca  
©2025 Government of Alberta  |  April 2025  |  Municipal Affairs 
  
STANDATA interpretation 19-EDI-013/23-BCI-038 
Elevating Devices/Building  
Firefighters’ Emergency Operation for alterations (FEO) 
Date Issued:  April 2025 
Page 1 of 7 
Purpose 
To clarify the requirements for Firefighters' Emergency Operations (FEO) when alterations are being made to 
existing elevating devices. 
Discussion 
Since 1975, there have been significant changes to the application for FEO in ASME A17.1/CSA B44, “Safety 
Code for Elevators and Escalators.” The changes continued to increase the level of safety for emergency 
responders and the public.  
Before the 1989 edition of ASME A17.1, there was no requirement for phase I or phase II FEO for an elevator 
with a rise of 50 feet or less. The 1989 to 1992 editions of ASME A17.1 required phase I for an elevator with a rise 
of 25 feet or more. In 1996, fire alarm initiating devices were introduced for automatic recall and designated 
landings. When an alteration to an elevator is undertaken in an existing building, the addition of FEO is always 
encouraged to support improved life safety for the occupants and emergency responders.  
The requirement for smoke detectors to be installed in elevator machine rooms and to recall the elevator upon 
actuation was first introduced in the Alberta Building Code 2014 (ABC 2014), which came into force May 1, 2015. 
The application of Elevator Emergency Return was introduced in the Alberta Building Code 1990 (ABC 1990), 
which came into force September 1, 1991.   
The National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) provides acceptable minimum standards for the 
construction and installation of life safety systems like FEO, including application of these standards when 
alterations are made to a building. While buildings are subject to the building code requirements in force at the 
time of construction, alterations typically are assessed as new construction and held to the standard of the current 
building code in f
---Application---
This joint interpretation applies to alterations of existing elevating devices in buildings where FEO was not 
required or installed at the time of installation.  
Interpretation 
1. The alterations Section 8.7 addresses multiple requirements that cover all aspects of the elevator. Under 
8.7.1.1, “Applicability of Alteration Requirements,” determining if FEO can be omitted requires review of the 
applicable building code and elevator code in force at the time of initial installation. During the permit 
process, the registered professional involved is to verify FEO was not required at the time of initial 
installation. ASME A17.3 is not adopted in Alberta, so clause 8.7.1.1(c) does not appl y.  
 
2. 8.7.2.27.4 and 8.7.3.31.5 apply to alterations to controllers. A controller with FEO capabilities is not 
considered an installed FEO system.  
 
3. If FEO is altered under 8.7.2.28 or 8.7.3.31.8, compliance with the requirements of 2.27.3. is to be followed. 
Under Sentence 1.1.1.2.(5) in Division A of the NBC(AE), the Building Safety Codes Officer (SCO) of the 
Authority Having Jurisdiction (AHJ) may accept an alternative or proposal that achieves the appropriate level 
of safety for the specific activity for which the building is to be used. 
 
4. Under Sentence 1.1.1.2.(5) in Division A of the NBC(AE), the Building Safety Codes Officer (SCO) of the 
AHJ may accept an alternative or proposal that achieves the appropriate level of safety for the specific 
activity for which the building is to be used.  
 
5. Where FEO is not required under 8.7.2.27.4 and 8.7.3.31.5, a Level 2 Elevating Devices SCO must review 
the alteration application and may request for a building SCO to review the alteration application. The 
building SCO and/or the Level 2 Elevating Devices SCO may attach conditions to the applicable permits for 
the alteration application.  
 
6. With reference to Sentence 1.1.1.2.(4) in Division A of the NBC(AE), the installation of an FEO elevator recall 
system is required, an FEO recall system designed by a registered professional is interpreted as an 
acceptable level of safety and building performance when an alteration is undertaken. 
 
7. If an elevator undergoes a subsequent alteration, the installation date referenced in 8.7.1.1(a) will be the date 
of the previous major alteration. FEO under 8.7.2.28 and 8.7.3.31.8 will apply.  
 
Note: Append

of the previous major alteration. FEO under 8.7.2.28 and 8.7.3.31.8 will apply.  
 
Note: Appendix “A” Flow Chart example

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-040 =====
 Building codes and standards | Alberta.ca 
©2025 Government of Alberta  |  May 2025  |  Municipal Affairs 
 
  
STANDATA interpretation 23-BCI-040 
Building  
  
Approved organization- APA – The Engineered Wood Association 
Date Issued:  May 2025 
Page 1 of 2 
Purpose  
To recognize an organization which issues evaluation reports used in determining compliance with the National 
Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) as being an approved organization in Alberta. 
Discussion 
The Canadian Construction Materials Centre (CCMC), National Research Council of Canada is already 
recognized within the NBC(AE) as an approved organization which issues evaluation reports which may be used 
in determining compliance with the NBC(AE).  
There are other third-party accredited organizations which also issue evaluation reports for the same purpose.  
These organizations will require the approval of the Provincial Building Administrator for their evaluation reports to 
be used in Alberta in accordance with Sentence 1.2.2.1.(2) of Division A of the NBC(AE).  
 
These organization are accredited by the Standards Council of Canada for certifying products where there are 
applicable standards. 
 
There may be new or innovative products for which a standard does not exist, and these products may be 
acceptable when supported by an evaluation report issued by an approved organization.  
Code References  
Division A 
1.2.2. Materials, Appliances, Systems and Equipment 
1.2.2.1. Characteristics of Materials, Appliances, Systems and Equipment 
 
1) All materials, appliances, systems and equipment installed to meet the requirements of this Code shall 
possess the necessary characteristics to perform their intended functions when installed in a building. 
 
2) Evaluation reports issued by the Canadian Construction Materials Centre, National Research Council of 
Canada, or an organization approved by the Provincial Building Administrator may be used in determining 
compliance with the requirements of this Code. 
 
 
  
STANDATA 23-BCI-040  Page 2 of 2 
Building codes and standards | 
---Application---
This interpretation applies to an organization that issue evaluation reports, for use in Alberta as provided for under 
the National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)). 
Interpretation 
This interpretation recognizes APA – The Engineered Wood Association as an approved organization that issue 
evaluation reports which may be used in determining compliance with the requirements of the NBC(AE). 
Conditions: 
1. The organization must maintain their accreditation with Standards Council of Canada  
2. Where there is a change or lapse of their accreditation the organization must notify the Provincial Building 
Administrator when such changes take effect.

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-042 =====
 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca  
Plumbing STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2025 Government of Alberta  |  July 2025  |  Municipal Affairs   
     
STANDATA interpretation 23-BCI-042/20-PCB-011R1 
Building/Plumbing  
  
Hydronic heating systems 
Date Issued:  J uly 2025 
Page 1 of 3 
Purpose 
This interpretation clarifies the design requirements of hydronic heating systems, so an acceptable level of 
performance may be maintained for all installations. 
Discussion 
The National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) prescribes heating and air-conditioning systems 
must be designed, constructed and installed to conform with “good practice.” While there are publications by 
industry and standards development organizations that are recognized by authorities having jurisdiction (AHJ) for 
use as “good practice,” safety codes officers (SCO) have asked for guidance regarding the process for verifying 
hydronic heating system has been designed, constructed, and installed according to the “good practice” 
documents referenced in the NBC(AE) 
For buildings within the scope of Division C, Sentence 2.4.1.1.(3) identifies full professional involvement is 
required on the project and, in the specific case of hydronic heating systems for these buildings, a registered 
engineering professional is usually retained to do the design and field review. 
Buildings outside the scope of Division C, Sentence 2.4.1.1.(3) are not required to have professionals involved in 
the design and field review process, unless the SCO is of the opinion that some or all aspects of the building 
design are complex enough to warrant professional involvement. For these buildings, SCOs  can use this 
interpretation as a guide to evaluate and review the design of hydronic heating systems submitted for a building 
permit. 
Code References  
Article 9.33.4.1. 
9.33.4.1. Design of Heating and Air-conditioning Systems 
1) Heating and air-conditioning systems, including ducting, and mechanical heating and refrigeration 
equipment, shall be designed, constructed and installed to conform with good practice such a
---Application---
This interpretation applies to any hydronic heating system intended to provide heat to the interior of the building. 
This includes systems designed as the primary heat source in the building, as well as any secondary heat 
sources (i.e. a house heated with a forced-air furnace having hydronic heating at the perimeter of a walk-out 
basement). Examples of hydronic heating systems would include, but not limited to: 
1. Poured-floor radiant tubing 
2. “Staple-up” radiant tubing 
3. Convective-plate in-floor tubing 
4. Radiators 
5. Baseboard heaters 
6. Fan-coil units 
 
This interpretation does not apply to systems not intended to provide heat to the interior of the building, such as 
exterior snowmelt systems or pool heaters. 
Interpretation 
Proper verification of compliance with Sentence 9.33.4.1.(1) for hydronic heating systems in buildings not within 
the scope of Division C, Sentence 2.4.1.1.(3) can be obtained by the following. 
DESIGN REQUIREMENTS 
Hydronic heating systems must be: 
1. Submitted as a pre-engineered package for review by the AHJ.  Pre-engineered packages must consist of the 
basic generic system specifications and installation details prepared by a registered engineering professional 
together with additional system design data and floor plans specific to the project. The additional system 
design must be completed by 
• A registered engineering professional licensed to practice in the province of Alberta; or  
• An individual holding one of the following qualifications: 
- A Certified Hydronics Designer, certified by the Canadian Hydronics Council, a council within the Canadian 
Institute for Plumbing and Heating; or 
- A Residential Hydronics Design Technician, certified by the Heating, Refrigeration and Air Conditioning 
Institute of Canada. 
2. Submitted as a custom design by 
• a registered engineering professional licensed to practice in the province of Alberta; or  
• an individual who holds one of the following qualifications: 
- A Certified Hydronics Designer, certified by the Canadian Hydronics Council; or  
- A Residential Hydronics Design Technician, certified by the Heating, Refrigeration and Air Conditioning 
Institute of Canada. 
STANDATA 23-BCI-042/20-PCB-011R1  Page 3 of 3

===== standata-nbc2023-interpretation-23-bci-043 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
Fire STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2025 Government of Alberta |  November 2025 | Municipal Affairs 
 
 
     
STANDATA interpretation 23-FCI-010/23-BCI-043 
Fire/Building  
  
 
Electromagnetic locks – installation, maintenance, testing, and verification  
Date Issued: November 2025 
Page 1 of 9 
Purpose 
To clarify how the Safety Codes Act (SCA), the National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) and the 
National Fire Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NFC(AE)) apply to the installation, maintenance, testing, and 
verification of electromagnetic locks. 
Discussion 
Electromagnetic locks (maglocks) use magnetic force to secure doors and are typically integrated with access 
control systems such as keypads, card readers, or remote release mechanisms. When energized, the magnet 
generates a strong holding force that keeps the door locked, making maglocks a common choice for security 
applications. However, maglocks must be integrated in a way that does not compromise fire protection systems or 
obstruct exit routes. Maglocks must be fail-safe, releasing upon fire alarm activation, power loss, or manual 
override, and must be compatible with fire-rated doors when used in such assemblies. They are often found in 
locations such as, but not limited to, commercial and industrial buildings, assemblies, banks, offices, hotels, high-
rises, institutions, mercantile occupancies, etc.  
Under the SCA, all stakeholders involved in the installation of maglocks have defined responsibilities to comply 
with the SCA and regulations pursuant to the SCA.  Under the SCA, appropriate permits must be obtained for any 
undertaking for which a permit is required and is applicable to maglocks.  
Maglock installations in new or existing buildings must be installed, tested, and verified in accordance with the 
applicable NBC(AE) provisions for safety, accessibility, and proper emergency egress. Article 2.2.3.3. Division C 
of the NBC(AE) requires fire alarm systems (FAS) plans to be submitted to the authority having jurisdiction (AHJ) 
for review. If the FAS i
---Application---
This interpretation applies to the installation, maintenance, testing, and verification of electromagnetic locks in 
both new and existing buildings. 
Interpretation 
This interpretation clarifies the requirements for installation, verification, inspection, testing, and maintenance for 
maglocks installed in a building connected to a FAS in new or existing buildings. Only individuals qualified under 
the NFC(AE) may perform these activities. 
 
1. Permits are required for installing maglocks, whether modifying an existing system or installing a new system. 
2. Where the NBC(AE) requires professional involvement due to a building’s size, occupancy, or complexity, a 
registered engineering professional must be retained to ensure compliance.  
  
STANDATA 23-FCI-010/23-BCI-043  Page 9 of 9 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
Fire STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2025 Government of Alberta |  November 2025 | Municipal Affairs 
 
 
3. Installation of maglocks in a building must comply with the NBC(AE).  
• Maglocks are recognized as an ancillary device requiring connection to the FAS. They must be: 
­ installed in accordance with the with CAN/ULC-S524, “Standard for Installation of Fire Alarm Systems.” 
­ verified in conformance with CAN/ULC-S537, “Standard for Verification of Fire Alarm Systems.” 
­ integrated testing must be completed in accordance with CAN/ULC-S1001, “Standard for Integrated 
Systems Testing of Fire Protection and Life Safety Systems”. 
4. Life safety systems must comply with the NFC(AE) throughout the building’s lifecycle. 
• Maglocks forming part of these systems must be: 
­ inspected and tested in conformance with CAN/ULC-S536, “Standard for Inspection and Testing of Fire 
Alarm Systems.”  
­ integrated testing must be tested and maintained in accordance with CAN/ULC-S1001, “Standard for 
Integrated Systems Testing of Fire Protection and Life Safety Systems”. 
5. Records of inspection, testing, and maintenance must be: 
• retained on-site.  
• made available to the AHJ upon request; and maintained in a format acceptable to the AHJ, as outlined 
in:  
­ CAN/ULC-S536, “Standard for Inspection and Testing of Fire Alarm Systems,”  
­ CAN/ULC-S537, “Standard for the Verification of Fire Alarm Systems,” or  
­ CAN/ULC-S1001, “Standard for Integrated Systems Testing of Fire Protection and Life Safety 
Systems.”

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-001 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2024 Government of Alberta  |  Published: April 2024 | 
 
     
STANDATA variance 23-BCV-001 
Building 
  
Window opening control devices (WOCD)  
Date Issued:  April 2024 
Page 1 of 3 
Purpose 
To provide an alternative solution in meeting the requirements of Sentences 9.8.8.1 (4) and (5) Guards on 
windows of residential occupancy in the National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC (AE)).  
Discussion 
The National Building Code - 2023 Alberta Edition introduced a new requirement in Clause 9.8.8.1 (4) (b) and 
Sentence 9.8.8.1.(5) by requiring a mechanism that can only be released with the use of tools or special 
knowledge to control the free swinging or sliding operation of all openable windows that are less than 900mm 
from the ﬁnished ﬂoor and more than 1800mm from the ﬂoor or ground level on the other side of the window. The 
intention of this requirement is to reduce the probability of  young children opening a window greater than 100mm 
and falling out of that window opening. 
The Appendix Note A-9.8.8.1 (4) refers to ASTM F2090 “Standard Speciﬁcation for Window Fall Prevention 
Devices with Emergency Escape (Egress) Release Mechanisms” as a way of providing the protection required in 
9.8.8.1 (4) (b) and (5). 
Also, Sentence 9.9.10.1 (1) requires that each bedroom must have at least one outside window or exterior door 
openable from the inside without the use of keys, tools, or special knowledge. However, there is no reference 
under Sentence 9.9.10.1.(1) to ASTM F2090 “Standard Speciﬁcation for Window Fall Prevention Devices with 
Emergency Escape (Egress) Release Mechanisms” as a means of providing the required window (openable with 
a minimum dimension of 380mm, and a minimum unobstructed area of .35m²). 
This variance allows for the installation of a mechanism that complies with ASTM F2090 “Standard Speciﬁcation for 
Window Fall Prevention Devices with Emergency Escape (Egress) Release Mechanisms” to be installed. 
Code References 
Sentences 9.8.8.1.(4) & (5) 
9.8.8.1. Required Guards 
4) Except as provided in S
---Application---
This variance applies to buildings of residential occupancy covered by Part 9 of the National Building Code – 
2023 Alberta Edition. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
STANDATA 23-BCV-001  Page 3 of 3 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2024 Government of Alberta  |  April 2024  |  Municipal Affairs 
 
 
Variance 
A WOCD mechanism that complies with ASTM F2090 “Standard Speciﬁcation for Window Fall Prevention 
Devices with Emergency Escape (Egress) Release Mechanisms” as a means of complying with the requirements 
of 9.8.8.1 (4) and 9.9.10.1 (1) with the following condition. 
1. WOCD mechanisms are not permitted for use in adaptable or barrier-free dwelling units. 
 
 This VARIANCE is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta. 
 
 
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are to Division B of the National Building Code  - 
2023 Alberta Edition

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-002 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2024 Government of Alberta  |  Published: May 2024  | Municipal Affairs 
 
     
STANDATA variance 23-BCV-002 
Building  
  
Review of off-site constructed buildings  
Date Issued:  May 2024  
Page 1 of 2 
Purpose 
To clarify the intent of off-site review as it applies to off-site construction of buildings. 
Discussion 
Off-site construction of buildings is a process of maximizing the delivery of building products as much as possible 
as one module/building. Currently it has become a well-suited solution to control project schedules and budgets 
while controlling quality and reducing environmental impact. 
Regulatory compliance of off-site construction is currently addressed under Division C, Sentence 2.5.1.1.(2) of the 
National Building Code - 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) by seeking certification from an accredited third party in 
accordance with CSA A277 “Procedure for certification of prefabricated buildings, modules, and panels”. Alberta 
recognizes the standard as confirming the building complies with the NBC(AE).  
Method of off-site review by registered professionals and/or inspections by the authority having jurisdiction (AHJ) 
achieves a minimum level of performance as required by the NBC(AE) in areas defined by the code objectives. 
Additionally, functional statements on the acceptable solution may be considered as an alternative to achieving 
code compliance.   
CSA-A660 “Certification of manufacturers of steel building systems” may be recognized for completed steel 
building modules. An additional letter of assurance by a registered professional or permit service report by an AHJ 
will be required for any installation of building services. (i.e., electrical, plumbing, HVAC, building envelope). 
Code References 
Division C, Article 2.5.1.1.  
2.5.1 Factory-Built Assemblies 
2.5.1.1. General 
1) Where a component of a building is assembled off the building site in such a manner that it cannot be 
reviewed on site, off-site reviews shall be carried out to determine compliance with this Code. 
 
2) Factory-constructed an
---Application---
This variance applies to off-site constructed buildings (except relocatable industrial camp buildings). 
Variance 
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE).  
 
The following alternative methods are deemed acceptable to ensure compliance with NBC(AE) for off-site 
constructed buildings: 
  
1. Certified in accordance with CSA A660 by an accredited organization with a “Certificate of Design and 
Manufacturing Conformance” for completed steel building modules. 
 
2. Verified by registered architectural/engineering professionals in accordance with Division C, Subsections 
2.4.2. and Section 2.5. of the NBC(AE). (i) 
 
3. Verified through inspections by the AHJ at the location of the work being undertaken. (ii) 
 
Notes:  
(i) Design drawings, specifications, and letter of conformance imprinted with the seals or stamps of a 
registered professional are required for this method. Also refer to the NBC(AE) defined term ‘field review’ 
and associated appendix note. 
(ii) If off-site inspection by the local authority where construction is carried out is not feasible, on- site 
inspection may be performed for critical items mutually agreed upon by the AHJ and permit applicant 
during the plan examination stage. This may be listed as a condition at the time the permit is issued. 
 
This VARIANCE is applicable throughout the province of Alberta.

2023 Alberta Edition

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-003 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2024 Government of Alberta  |  Published: May 2024  | Municipal Affairs 
 
     
STANDATA variance 23-BCV-003 
Building 
 
  
Insulation below a heated floor 
Date Issued:  May 2024 
Page 1 of 3 
Purpose 
To provide an alternative solution for providing adequate insulation value below a heated floor in contact with the 
ground. 
Discussion 
The objective of insulation below a heated floor in contact with the ground is to reduce the environmental impact 
due to heat transmission to the ground from the heated floor. Section 9.36. of the National Building Code –  2023 
Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) ensures this objective is achieved. However, this code provision impacts the viability of 
hydronic heating systems in floors in contact with the ground. Hydronic heat is an energy efficient method for 
heating a home that uses tubing to run a hot liquid in the floor, along base board heaters or through radiators to 
heat your home. Also referred to as radiant heating, this type of system has become increasingly popular in 
Alberta for homeowners who want added comfort and control in their heating zones, savings through lower 
heating bills, and a decrease in their environmental impact by making smart green building choices.  
Current under slab-insulation code provisions do not take into account hydronic heating systems as an 
appropriate method of adequate insulation value below a heated floor in contact with the ground. Municipal Affairs 
identified that the provisions for current slab-insulation are more than what is required to meet energy efficiency 
objectives and is consulting with the National Research Council (NRC) to recommend an appropriate insulation 
rating that will allow for hydronic heating installations while also supporting Alberta’s energy efficiency goals. This 
province-wide variance will allow an alternative solution to the provisions under Section 9.36. for use in 
installations below heated floors in contact with the ground. It is important to note, this variance will not remove 
the insulation requirement from Section 
---Application---
This variance applies to Part 9 buildings where a heating system creates a heated floor in contact with the 
ground, either fully below the frost line or above the frost line. 
Variance 
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE). 
1. A minimum RSI 1.32 (R 7.5) under slab insulation complies when installed for a heated floor in contact with 
the ground.  
This VARIANCE is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta. 
 
 
 
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are to Division B of the National Building Code -  
2023 Alberta Edition.  
Issued by the Provincial Building Administrator

Paul Chang 
Alberta Municipal Affairs – Technical and Corporate Services 
Email: safety.services@gov.ab.ca  Phone: 1-866-421-6929 
To sign up for our List Subscription Service go to: municipalaffairs.gov.ab.ca/am_list_subscription_services  
 
---Variance---
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE). 
1. A minimum RSI 1.32 (R 7.5) under slab insulation complies when installed for a heated floor in contact with 
the ground.

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-004 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2024 Government of Alberta  |  May 2024  |  Municipal Affairs 
 
 
     
STANDATA variance 23-BCV-004 
Building 
 
  
Sprinkler-protected glazing in fire-resistant wall assemblies 
Date Issued:  May 2024 
Page 1 of 3 
Purpose 
To set conditions where the use of sprinkler-protected glazing assemblies in interior and exterior fire rated wall 
assemblies is acceptable. 
 
Discussion  
Sentence 3.1.7.1.(1) of the National Building Code - 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) specifies the fire-resistance 
rating of a material, assembly, or structural component must be determined by testing to CAN/ULC-S101, 
“Standard Method of Fire Endurance Tests of Building Construction and Materials.” 
Designers want to install clear glass in fire separations and  exit enclosures to provide greater visibility within the 
building. Glass block and wired glass panels do not provide the visibility that designers would like to incorporate 
into their designs. As such, an alternative to using glass block and wired glass panels has been requested by 
stakeholders. 
Sprinkler-protected glazing is a design alternative that has been in use for years. It varies slightly from the concept 
of using glass block and wired glass panels . Sprinkler-protected glazing is not considered as a closure in a fire-
rated assembly. Instead, the fire- resistance rating assigned to the combination of glazing assembly and sprinkler 
heads must be able to achieve at least the same fire- resistance rating of the fire separation it is being installed 
within. ULC-S101 does not contain requirements that adequately address sprinkler -protected glazing. 
Since the sprinkler-protected glazing is used as an alternative to the traditional fire -rated assembly, the installation 
of the sprinkler-protected glazing panels does not reduce the number of unprotected openings permitted in a given 
exterior wall assembly. Consequently, glazing assemblies are permitted to be installed for interior or exterior fire 
rated assemblies required to have a fire- resistance rating without  being tested to CA
---Variance---
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE). 
Glazing assemblies are permitted to be installed for interior or exterior fire rated assemblies that are required to 
have a fire-resistance rating without being tested to CAN/ULC-S101, “Standard Method of Fire Endurance Tests 
of Building Construction and Materials” as required by Sentences 3.1.7.1.(1) and 9.10.3.1.(1) provided they are 
protected by a fixed, automatic sprinkler system and the following criteria are met: 
 
1. The assembly has been evaluated by a testing agency accredited by the Standards Council of Canada for 
conformance to CAN/ULC-S136:2021, “Standard Method of Fire Test for Sprinkler Protected Window 
Systems”. 
2. The design, construction and installation of automatic sprinkler system conforms to NFPA 13. 
3. Sprinklers are listed as specific application window sprinklers. 
4. Glazing is heat-strengthened, tempered, or glass ceramic and fixed. 
5. Where the assembly is required to be protected from both sides, sprinklers to  be installed on both sides of the 
glazing. 
6. If the assembly is located in a loadbearing assembly, all loadbearing components have a fire- resistance rating 
not less than that required for the supported assembly. 
7. The glazed assembly does not include any horizontal members interfering with uniform distribution of water 
over the surface of the glazing, and there are no obstructions between sprinklers and glazing that would 
obstruct water distribution. 
8. The fire-resistance rating of the sprinkler-protected glazing system is equal to or greater than the fire-
resistance rating required for the wall assembly being installed within, and the sprinkler-protected glazed wall 
assemblies constructed according to the requirements of the listing of the window sprinkler. 
 
STANDATA 23-BCV-004  Page 3 of 3 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2024 Government of Alberta  |  May 2024  |  Municipal Affairs 
 
 
9. The assembly is not installed in: 
• a firewall or within a door installed in a firewall, 
• a building that contains a high hazard industrial occupancy, 
• a location where there is a risk of explosion, or 
• any part of a required exit serving: 
- a floor area subjected to the additional requirements for high buildings as per Subsection 3

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-005 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca

STANDATA variance 23-BCV-005 
Building 
 
  
Occupancy classification of oil and gas processing buildings 
Date Issued:  May 2024 
Page 1 of 3 
Purpose 
To allow oil and gas Group F, Division 1 hig- hazard industrial occupancies to be classified as Group F, Division 2 
medium-hazard occupancy under certain conditions as an alternative solution under the National Building Code - 
2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)).  
Discussion 
Oil and gas processing buildings are often classified as Group F, Division 1, high- hazard industrial occupancies 
thus requiring these sites to comply with more restrictive provisions. In oil and gas facilities, the work environment, 
safe work procedures, and worker safety training is an important complement to the building’s fire protection and 
suppression systems.  
Oil and gas companies have developed guides describing design and operation practices that support safe 
working conditions at small, portable oil and gas production facilities, often classified as high hazard industrial 
occupancies. The objective of these guides is to limit the probability that a person in or  adjacent to a facility may 
be exposed to an unacceptable level of risk of injury or illness. 
The Canadian Association of Petroleum Producers (CAPP) recently produced “Safety Guidelines for Small, 
Portable Oil and Gas Production Facilities: Recommended Solutions for Design and Operations ”. The guide is 
intended to supplement other applicable standards and regulatory codes including the NBC(AE). It recommends 
solutions for safe design, operation, and maintenance practices for field facilities including worker training and 
competency. There are three main areas of focus: process safety, public safety, and occupational health and 
safety. Owners should assess their own requirements to establish design, operating, maintenance, and w ork. 
Municipal Affairs reviewed the CAPP guide and found the implementation of the recommended guidelines, in 
addition to o
---Application---
 This variance applies to oil or gas processing buildings that are: 
 
1. considered as low human occupancy buildings, 
2. located outside of urban areas, and 
3. used to house oil and gas processing equipment, such as but not limited to: 
• compressor stations, 
• heater packages, 
• pump packages, 
• separator packages, 
• treater packages, 
• dehydrator units, 
• field equipment, 
• L.P.G. handling facilities, 
• refrigeration process units, 
• oil batteries, and 
• similar types of buildings. 
This variance does not apply to residential buildings, office buildings, warehousing, and other buildings not 
specifically used for oil or gas processing even though these buildings may be located on the same site.  
Variance 
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE). 
 
Oil or gas processing buildings may be classified as Group F, Division 2 medium- hazard occupancy provided: 
1. The building does not exceed 150 m2 in building area.  
2. A risk assessment has been prepared by a Registered Engineering Professional, licensed to practice in the  
province of Alberta, which includes: 
• The determination of adequate rates of gravity and/or mechanical ventilation based on  
- requirements for electrical area classification and recommendations given in API RP500, 
“Recommended Practice for Classification of Locations for Electrical Installations at Petroleum 
Facilities as Class I, Division 1 and Division 2” for normal or continuous operation,  
- a minimum of six air changes per hour, or 
- higher ventilation rates where sour gas may be present. 
• The installation of combustible and/or toxic gas detection, 
• The installation of emergency shut down stations with remote activators, 
• The installation of listed fire detection equipment (which may include flame detectors in lieu of smoke or heat 
detectors) to automatically shut down and depressurize the facility, and

• The provision of a supply of water for external fire fighting for buildings in remote areas.

permit application submission. 
3.

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-006 =====
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca 
©2024 Government of Alberta  |  Published: May 2024  | Municipal Affairs 
 
     
STANDATA variance 23-BCV-006 
Building 
 
  
Occupancy classification of natural gas regulating and metering buildings 
Date Issued:  May 2024  
Page 1 of 2 
Purpose 
To provide an alternative solution for occupancy classification of Group F, Division 1 high -hazard industrial 
occupancies that house natural gas and metering equipment to be classified as Group F, Division 2 medium-
hazard occupancy under certain conditions. 
Discussion 
High Hazard Industrial Occupancy (Group F, Division 1) is defined by the National Building Code -  2023 Alberta 
Edition (NBC(AE)) as an industrial occupancy containing sufficient quantities of highly combustible, flammable or 
explosive materials which, because of their inherent characteristics, may constitute a special fire hazard.  
Buildings containing natural gas regulating and metering equipment are typically designed to protect the 
equipment. These buildings are not accessible by the public and are only intended for occupancy by employees 
for maintenance so the risk of harm to the public is lower than other buildings. Such features allow for accepting 
Group F, Division 2 medium-hazard occupancy as an alternative occupancy classification.   
Code References  
Sentence 6.3.1.5.(1)  
6.3.1.5. Indoor Air Contaminants  
(See Note A-6.3.1.5.)  
1) Air contaminants of concern that are released within buildings shall  
a) be removed insofar as is possible at their points of origin, and  
b) not be permitted to accumulate in concentrations greater than those permitted by applicable 
provincial or territorial requirements or, in the absence of such requirements, by good engineering 
practice such as that described in the publications listed in Sentence 6.2.1.1.(1), measured using the 
methodology described therein. 
 
Division A, Article 1.4.1.2.  
1.4.1.2. Defined Terms 
High-hazard industrial occupancy (Group F, Division 1) means an industrial occupancy containing 
sufficient quantities of highly combustible and flamm
---Application---
This variance applies to Group F, Division 1  high-hazard industrial occupancies housing natural gas and 
metering equipment. 
 
Variance 
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE).  
Buildings used to house natural gas regulating and metering equipment may be classified as Group F, Division 2 
medium-hazard occupancy provided: 
1. the building does not exceed 55 m2 in building area or one storey in building height,  
2. the building is considered as a low human occupancy building, and  
3. sufficient ventilation is available as per Sentence 6.3.1.5.(1) so any small leaks from fittings will not 
accumulate and there is a constant movement of air. 
 
This VARIANCE is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are to Division B of the National Building Code - 
2023 Alberta Edition 
Issued by the Provincial Building Administrator

---Variance---
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE).  
Buildings used to house natural gas regulating and metering equipment may be classified as Group F, Division 2 
medium-hazard occupancy provided: 
1. the building does not exceed 55 m2 in building area or one storey in building height,  
2. the building is considered as a low human occupancy building, and  
3. sufficient ventilation is available as per Sentence 6.3.1.5.(1) so any small leaks from fittings will not 
accumulate and there is a constant movement of air. 
 
This VARIANCE is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta.

STANDATA variance 23-BCV-007 
Building 
 
  
Maximum span values for joists, rafters and beams  
Date Issued:  May 2024 
Page 1 of 2 
Purpose 
 
To recognize the acceptability of the values within the “The Span Book” published by the Canadian Wood Council  
(CWC). 
 
Discussion 
 
Article 9.23.4.2. of the National Building Code - 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) references the span tables 
located at the end of Part 9 for determining the maximum permissible spans of joists, rafters , and beams under 
various loading conditions. These tables are formulated by the CWC on behalf of the National Research Council 
of Canada, as noted in the Appendix Note A-9.23.4.2. 
 
Code References 
 
Article 9.23.4.2. 
9.23.4.2. Spans for Joists, Rafters and Beams 
(See Note A-9.23.4.2.) 
1) Except as required in Sentence (2) and Article 9.23.14.10., spans for wood joists  and rafters shall conform to 
the spans shown in Span Tables 9.23.4.2.-A to 9.23.4.2.-G for the uniform live loads shown in the Tables. (See 
Article 9.4.2.2.) 
2) Spans for floor joists that are not selected from Span Tables 9.23.4.2.-A and 9.23.4.2.-B and that are 
required to be designed for the same loading conditions, shall not exceed the design requirements for uniform 
loading and vibration criteria. 
(See Note A-9.23.4.2.(2).) 
3) Spans for built-up wood and glued-laminated timber floor beams shall conform to the spans in Span Tables 
9.23.4.2.-H to 9.23.4.2.-K. (See Article 9.4.2.2.) 
 
 
Appendix Note A-9.23.4.2. 
A-9.23.4.2. Span Tables for Wood Joists, Rafters and Beams. 
The Canadian Wood Council publishes “The Span Book,” a compilation of span tables covering 
many of these alternative configurations. Although these tables have not been subject to the 
formal committee review process, the Canadian Wood Council generates, for the CCBFC, all of 
the Code's span tables for wood structural components; thus Code users can be confident that 
the alternative span tables in “The Span Book” are con
---Application---
This variance applies to the alternative configuration of joists, rafters, and beams available in ‘The Span Book’ 
published by CWC. 
Variance 
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE). 
 
The values for the maximum spans of joists, rafters, and beams referenced in Article 9.23.4.2. of the NBC(AE) are 
permitted to be determined using the values from: 
The Span Book (2020 Edition) 
ISBN: 978-1-989039-06-9

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-008-rev1 =====
 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca

STANDATA variance 23-BCV-008(REV1) 
Building 
  
Residential exhaust outlet clearance from air intakes 
Date Issued: October 2024 
Page 1 of 4 
Purpose 
Provides an alternative solution to Sentence 9.32.3.13.(4) for the minimum clearance requirement for domestic 
exhaust outlets that discharge air containing moisture, such as kitchen, bathroom, and clothes dryer exhaust 
outlets, from air intakes. 
Discussion 
The National Building Code of Canada 2020 introduced changes to Article 9.32.3.13. for minimum clearance 
requirements related to exhaust vents, soffits, and air intakes. The National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition 
(NBC(AE)) subsequently harmonized with these changes.  
The minimum clearance required between a gas vent or oil fill pipe and a mechanically vented air intake was 
increased from 900 mm to 1 800 mm in Sentence 9.32.3.13.(3).  
Additionally, new Sentences (4) to (6) were introduced to address the minimum clearance required from exhaust 
outlets discharging moisture-laden air to soffits and air intakes. While the national code change rationale for 
Sentences (4) to (6) specifically explained the application of the 1 800 mm minimum clearance requirement to 
soffits, it did not address the application to air intakes as provided in Sentence (4). A required 1 800 mm minimum 
clearance between moist-air exhaust outlets and air intakes for domestic applications was not explained and other 
standards suggest it can be potentially reduced. 
CSA-B149.1, “Natural gas and propane installation code,” (CSA B149.1) provides minimum clearances between 
clothes dryer vent terminations and air intakes: 900 mm for domestic clothes dryers and 1 800 mm for commercial 
clothes dryers. As domestic clothes dryer exhaust air typically contains more moisture than bathroom or kitchen 
exhaust air, this supports the basis for a variance that reduces the 1 800 mm minimum required clearance 
between a moist-air exhaust outlet and air intake specified by Sentence 
---Application---
This variance applies to exhaust outlets that discharge air containing moisture, such as kitchen, bathroom and 
clothes dryer exhaust outlets, serving an individual dwelling unit or a house with a secondary suite.  
Variance 
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE).   
1. The minimum clearances separating exhaust outlets that discharge air containing moisture from air intakes 
are permitted to comply with Figure A. 
 
 
 
Figure A

STANDATA variance 23-BCV-008 
Building 
Residential exhaust outlet clearance from air intakes 
Date Issued:  June 2024 
Page 1 of 3 
Purpose 
To provide an alternative solution to clearance requirements between domestic kitchen, bathroom  and clothes 
dryer exhaust outlets from air intakes. 
Discussion 
The requirements for exhaust clearances from soffits were updated in the National Building Code of Canada 2020 
and subsequently harmonized within the National Building Code - 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)). The 
requirements prescribed a clearance of 1800 mm from exhaust vents below soffits or to install solid soffit s where 
the clearance is less than 1800 mm below soffits. 
Clearance was inc
reased from 900 mm to 1800 mm for clothes dryer exhaust vents to air intakes. This change 
did not differentiate between clothes dryers in common laundry rooms serving residential buildings or domestic 
clothes dryers serving a single dwelling unit or a house with a secondary suite. 
Clearance o
f 1800 mm was also added for bathroom ventilation exhaust from air intakes.  
The rati
onale presented within the requirements for exhaust clearances explained the application to soffits but 
did not provide a rationale applicable to clothes dryer exhaust or bathroom exhaust from air intakes. The 
rationale also describes “air containing moisture rises from the exhaust outlets” as part of the problem. The 
rationale was silent on kitchen exhaust from dwelling units. But to clarify are also addressing kitchen exhaust 
outlets. 
Kitchen,
 bathroom, and clothes dryer exhaust serving individual dwelling units or houses with a secondary suite 
typically operate intermittently.  
CSA-B149.
1 “Natural gas and propane installation code” (CSA B149.1) provides for vent terminations from 
building air intakes prescribing a clearance of 900 mm for domestic clothes dryers and 1800 mm for commercial 
clothes dryers. This supports the allowance for the clearance reduction when dealing with domestic clothes
---Application---
This variance applies to kitchen, bathroom, and clothes dryer exhaust outlets serving individual dwelling units or a 
house with a secondary suite.  
Variance 
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE).   
1. The horizontal clearance of kitchen, bathroom and clothes dryer exhaust outlets is permitted to be within 900
mm  from air intakes serving the individual dwelling unit or house with a secondary suite.

e: Air intake is understood to be connected to a fan.

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-009 =====
 
 
Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca  
©2024 Government of Alberta  |  October 2024  |  Municipal Affairs 
 
 
     
STANDATA variance 23-BCV-009 
Building 
  
CSA B52:23 Mechanical refrigeration code 
Date Issued:  October 2024 
Page 1 of 2 
Purpose 
Allows the acceptance of CSA B52:23, “Mechanical refrigeration code,” under the National Building Code – 2023 
Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)).  
Discussion 
On January 1, 2025, new standards for manufacturing with refrigerants will go into effect.  
 
These standards include prohibiting the manufacture of products containing certain hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs) by 
January 1, 2025. HFCs are synthetic organic compounds used in air conditioning and as refrigerants.   
 
To conform to these rule changes, the 2023 edition of the CSA B52 code introduced the A2L and B2L refrigerant 
groups as more environmentally friendly refrigerants. Equipment manufacturers have also begun to adapt to the 
rule changes by re-tooling their factories to manufacture new equipment to allow for the use of these A2L and B2L 
refrigerant groups. Additionally, there is already equipment on the market which use the new refrigerants.  
 
The NBC(AE), however, currently references the 2018 edition of the CSA B52 Code, as per Table 1.3.1.2. of 
Division B. To align with the HFC prohibition and industry’s change in supply of HVAC equipment across Canada, 
it is necessary for the NBC(AE) to recognize the CSA B52:23 Code. 
 
Code References 
Sentence 6.2.1.5.(1) 
6.2.1.5. Installation Standards 
1) Except as provided in Articles 6.9.4.2. and 6.3.1.4., the installation of heating and air-conditioning 
equipment, including mechanical refrigeration equipment, and including provisions for mounting, 
clearances and air supply, shall conform to the requirements of 
a) the Pressure Equipment Safety Regulation made pursuant to the Safety Codes Act,  
b) CSA B52, “Mechanical refrigeration code,” 
c) CSA B139 Series, “Installation code for oil-burning equipment,” 
d) the Gas Code Regulation made pursuant to the Safety Codes Act, 
e) CSA B365, “Installation Code for Solid-
---Application---
This variance applies to the installation of equipment required to conform to CSA B52, as per Sentences 
6.2.1.5.(1) and 9.33.5.2.(1) of the NBC(AE). 
Variance 
This variance provides approximately equivalent or greater safety performance with respect to persons and 
property as provided for by the Safety Codes Act and the NBC(AE). 
 
1. Conformance with CSA B52:23, “Mechanical refrigeration code,” is considered to comply with the 
installation standard requirements of the NBC(AE). 
This VARIANCE is applicable throughout the Province of Alberta. 
 
 
Unless stated otherwise, all Code references in this STANDATA are to Division B of the National Building Code - 
2023 Alberta Edition 
Issued by the Provincial Building Administrator 
 
 
[Original signed] 
Paul Chang

===== standata-nbc2023-variance-23-bcv-010 =====
 
 Building STANDATA | Alberta.ca   
©2025 Government of Alberta  |  February 2025   |  Municipal Affairs  
 
STANDATA variance 23-BCV-010 
Building 
Engineer authentication for the qualification of structural steel fabricators  
Date Issued:   February 2025 
Page 1 of 5 
Purpose 
To provide an alternative solution to the industry in meeting the requirement of using qualified fabricators for the 
fabrication of structural steel as per CSA W47.1 – “Certification of companies for fusion welding of steel”.  
Discussion  
Structural steel is at times fabricated outside of Canada by fabricators who are not certified to the CSA W47.1 
standard. The majority of fabricators in Canada are certified to the CSA W47.1 standard. However, it is not common 
to find CSA W47.1 qualified fabricators outside of Canada.    
 
The standard commonly adopted by the international community of fabricators involved in the fabrication and 
welding of structural steel is AWS D1.1 “Structural Welding Code–Steel”. The Canadian equivalent standard to 
AWS D1.1 is CSA W59 “Welded steel construction” (metal arc welding). The two standards are quite similar in the 
welding requirements. The CSA W47.1 “Certification of companies for fusion welding of steel”, is associated with 
CSA W59, which provides certification requirements to structural steel fabricators. However, there is no equivalent 
standard to complement CSA W47.1 since the industry relies on fabricators’ own quality programs to comply with 
the AWS D1.1 requirements during welding activities.      
 
The National Building Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (NBC(AE)) mandates the use of CSA S16 “ Design of steel 
structures” and Canadian Welding Bureau (CWB) certification.  
 
The requirement to comply with CSA W47.1 stems from CSA S16 (refer to Clause 24.3) which is mandated by the 
NBC(AE). In scenarios which involve fabricators outside of Canada due to limitations of access to CWB certified 
companies, allowance should be made available for industry to utilize this variance.  This variance provides an 
analysis of AWS D1.1 as an alternative 
---Application---
This variance applies to the fabrication of steel structures that are fabricated outside of Canada for its installation  
in the province of Alberta.  
The following comparison-table summarizes the differences between CSA W47.1 and AWS D1.1 and provides 
measures to achieve equivalency when a fabricator  is not certified to CSA W47.1 or CWB. 
A project specific Quality Management System and Welding Program as mutually agreed between the registered 
engineering professional  and the owner must  be used for the evaluation and assessment of the fabricator.  
The registered engineering professional must perform an assessment using the table as a tool and document 
each aspect of the below mentioned comparison-table to demonstrate compliance for the alternative solution of 
AWS D1.1.

---Variance---
Where a non-CSA W47.1 certified fabricator  is involved in the fabrication of structural steel , the owner is 
responsible for the authentication process of the welding program, including the review of weld procedures by a 
registered engineering professional knowledgeable in welding procedures  and techniques . The authenticated 
thing, process, or activity is considered to provide approximately equivalent or greater safety performance subject 
to the following conditions:  
1. A Quality Management System is in place. 
2. A Welding Program is in place, which includes, but is not limited to the following : 
• Qualified Weld Procedures suitable for the scope of work ,  
• Qualified Welders ,  
• Welding Foreman, Welding Supervisor , or Registered Engineering Professional, and   
• Procedure for the Management of Welding Consumables .  
3. A uthenticated documents are submitted to the Authority Having Jurisdiction : 
• APEGA’s reference guideline - Authenticating Professional Work Products . 
 
This VARIANCE is applicable throughout the P rovince of Alberta.
